It’s more just how it looks. I keep seeing this rough, kind of sunken patch in the mirror or in photos, like one side of my face looks older or beat-up. But I have been late getting out the door for construction jobs because I keep checking it or trying to get myself looking… normal, I guess.\n\nAnd I’ve started dodging site photos when they come up. I also skipped a couple family game nights, which feels dumb to say, just because I didn’t want everybody looking at my face. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about your face focused on this one patch, or do you also worry about other parts of your appearance?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly that one patch, yeah. I mean, I don’t think I’m picking apart everything else… at least not like that. It’s just that rough, kind of sunken-looking spot on my face. I keep seeing it in the mirror and in photos, like one side of me looks older or beat-up.\n\nPeople keep telling me they barely notice anything, so I know it probably sounds like I’m making too much of it, but that’s the thing I can’t seem to stop focusing on. Is that… does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that others can see this patch on your face as clearly as you can?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I don’t know. I feel like I can see it really clearly, especially in photos or when I catch myself in the mirror. It looks rough and kind of sunken to me, like that side of my face looks older or beat-up.\n\nBut other people keep saying they barely notice anything, so… maybe they don’t see it the way I do. I’m trying not to make too big a deal out of it, but it’s hard for me not to focus on it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I’m not trying to be dramatic about it. It just feels very real when I see it, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the patch on your face is not as noticeable as you think it is, even when you're looking at it yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it’s possible. But I have been late getting out the door for construction jobs because I keep checking it or trying to get myself looking… normal, I guess.\n\nAnd I’ve started dodging site photos when they come up. I also skipped a couple family game nights, which feels dumb to say, just because I didn’t want everybody looking at my face. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about your face focused on this one patch, or do you also worry about other parts of your appearance?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly that one patch, yeah. I mean, I don’t think I’m picking apart everything else… at least not like that. It’s just that rough, kind of sunken-looking spot on my face. I keep seeing it in the mirror and in photos, like one side of me looks older or beat-up.\n\nPeople keep telling me they barely notice anything, so I know it probably sounds like I’m making too much of it, but that’s the thing I can’t seem to stop focusing on. Is that… does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that others can see this patch on your face as clearly as you can?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I don’t know. I feel like I can see it really clearly, especially in photos or when I catch myself in the mirror. It looks rough and kind of sunken to me, like that side of my face looks older or beat-up.\n\nBut other people keep saying they barely notice anything, so… maybe they don’t see it the way I do. I’m trying not to make too big a deal out of it, but it’s hard for me not to focus on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your perception of this patch on your face is accurate, or could it be that you're seeing something that isn't really there?\n\nPatient: I mean… I want to say it’s accurate, because when I look in the mirror or see a photo, it really does look rough and kind of sunken to me. Like that side of my face looks older, or beat-up.\n\nBut then people keep telling me they barely notice anything, so… I guess it’s possible I’m seeing it bigger than it really is. I’m not trying to be dramatic about it. It just feels very real when I see it, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the patch on your face is not as noticeable as you think it is, even when you're looking at it yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it’s possible. I mean, I’m here because I can’t seem to stop seeing it, that rough, kind of sunken patch, especially in pictures or when I’m looking in the mirror. To me it makes one side of my face look old or beat-up.\n\nBut I know people keep saying they barely notice anything, so maybe I’m seeing it more than other people would… or making it stand out more in my own head. I don’t know. I’m trying to be reasonable about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the patch on your face isn't actually there at all?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that without sounding unreasonable. It feels like it’s there to me. I see this rough, kind of sunken-looking patch when I look in the mirror or in photos, and it makes one side of my face look older or beat-up.\n\nBut I get what you’re asking. Since other people keep saying they barely notice anything, I guess there’s a possibility I’m seeing something that isn’t really there the way I think it is. I’m trying to be honest about that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the patch on your face isn't actually visible to anyone else, even if you're convinced you see it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess it’s possible. I don’t want to sound like I’m arguing with everybody, because people do keep telling me they barely notice anything.\n\nIt’s just when I look in the mirror, or especially in photos, I can’t stop seeing that rough, kind of sunken-looking patch. To me it makes one side of my face look old and beat-up. But maybe… maybe it isn’t really visible to other people the way it is to me. I’m trying to keep that in mind.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I want to say yes, because I know that’s probably the healthier answer. If enough people, especially doctors, told me there’s nothing there, I’d try to believe them.

But… I don’t know. I’m here because I can’t stop seeing it. In the mirror, in photos, my eye goes right to this rough, kind of sunken-looking patch, and it makes one side of my face look old and beat-up to me. Other people keep saying they barely notice anything, so maybe I’m making it bigger than it is.

I guess I’d need to really be able to look and not see it myself. I don’t mean to sound difficult. It’s just hard to imagine that right now.

I keep seeing it in the mirror and in photos, like one side of me looks older or beat-up.\n\nPeople keep telling me they barely notice anything, so I know it probably sounds like I’m making too much of it, but that’s the thing I can’t seem to stop focusing on. I feel like I can see it really clearly, especially in photos or when I catch myself in the mirror. It looks rough and kind of sunken to me, like that side of my face looks older or beat-up.\n\nBut other people keep saying they barely notice anything, so… maybe they don’t see it the way I do. I’m trying not to make too big a deal out of it, but it’s hard for me not to focus on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your perception of this patch on your face is accurate, or could it be that you're seeing something that isn't really there?\n\nPatient: I mean… I want to say it’s accurate, because when I look in the mirror or see a photo, it really does look rough and kind of sunken to me. I’m not trying to be dramatic about it. It just feels very real when I see it, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the patch on your face is not as noticeable as you think it is, even when you're looking at it yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it’s possible.     "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF45.2, F48.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… it’s probably not the biggest thing, but I’ve been getting kind of stuck on this patch on my face. In the mirror and especially in photos it looks rough and sort of sunken to me, like one side of my face looks old or beat-up. People tell me they barely notice anything, so maybe I’m making too much of it, but I can’t seem to stop seeing it.\n\nDoctor: Is it specifically the appearance of this patch on your face that bothers you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, it sounds kind of silly when I say it, but it’s that rough, sunken-looking spot on my face. I keep noticing it in the mirror and in pictures, like that side of my face looks older or beat-up somehow. Other people say they barely see anything, so maybe I’m overdoing it, but that’s what I keep getting stuck on.\n\nDoctor: Is it only the way this patch looks that bothers you, or are you also worried about what it might mean for your health?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t really think it’s a health thing… at least, that’s not what I’m focused on. It’s more just how it looks. I keep seeing this rough, kind of sunken patch in the mirror or in photos, like one side of my face looks older or beat-up. I know people say they barely notice it, so maybe I’m making it bigger than it is, but that’s what keeps bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Has this concern about the patch on your face been affecting your daily life or interactions with others in any way?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I’m still getting to work and everything, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But I have been late getting out the door for construction jobs because I keep checking it or trying to get myself looking… normal, I guess.\n\nAnd I’ve started dodging site photos when they come up. I also skipped a couple family game nights, which feels dumb to say, just because I didn’t want everybody looking at my face. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about your face focused on this one patch, or do you also worry about other parts of your appearance?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly that one patch, yeah. I mean, I don’t think I’m picking apart everything else… at least not like that. It’s just that rough, kind of sunken-looking spot on my face. I keep seeing it in the mirror and in photos, like one side of me looks older or beat-up.\n\nPeople keep telling me they barely notice anything, so I know it probably sounds like I’m making too much of it, but that’s the thing I can’t seem to stop focusing on. Is that… does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that others can see this patch on your face as clearly as you can?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I don’t know. I feel like I can see it really clearly, especially in photos or when I catch myself in the mirror. It looks rough and kind of sunken to me, like that side of my face looks older or beat-up.\n\nBut other people keep saying they barely notice anything, so… maybe they don’t see it the way I do. I’m trying not to make too big a deal out of it, but it’s hard for me not to focus on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your perception of this patch on your face is accurate, or could it be that you're seeing something that isn't really there?\n\nPatient: I mean… I want to say it’s accurate, because when I look in the mirror or see a photo, it really does look rough and kind of sunken to me. Like that side of my face looks older, or beat-up.\n\nBut then people keep telling me they barely notice anything, so… I guess it’s possible I’m seeing it bigger than it really is. I’m not trying to be dramatic about it. It just feels very real when I see it, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the patch on your face is not as noticeable as you think it is, even when you're looking at it yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it’s possible. I mean, I’m here because I can’t seem to stop seeing it, that rough, kind of sunken patch, especially in pictures or when I’m looking in the mirror. To me it makes one side of my face look old or beat-up.\n\nBut I know people keep saying they barely notice anything, so maybe I’m seeing it more than other people would… or making it stand out more in my own head. I don’t know. I’m trying to be reasonable about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the patch on your face isn't actually there at all?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that without sounding unreasonable. It feels like it’s there to me. I see this rough, kind of sunken-looking patch when I look in the mirror or in photos, and it makes one side of my face look older or beat-up.\n\nBut I get what you’re asking. Since other people keep saying they barely notice anything, I guess there’s a possibility I’m seeing something that isn’t really there the way I think it is. I’m trying to be honest about that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the patch on your face isn't actually visible to anyone else, even if you're convinced you see it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess it’s possible. I don’t want to sound like I’m arguing with everybody, because people do keep telling me they barely notice anything.\n\nIt’s just when I look in the mirror, or especially in photos, I can’t stop seeing that rough, kind of sunken-looking patch. To me it makes one side of my face look old and beat-up. But maybe… maybe it isn’t really visible to other people the way it is to me. I’m trying to keep that in mind.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still see this patch on your face if multiple doctors told you there was nothing there?\n\nPatient: I’d like to say I’d just accept it, because I know that would be the reasonable thing to do… but honestly, I think I might still see it.\n\nMaybe I’d try to tell myself the doctors are right, and maybe it’s not something other people can really notice. But when I look in the mirror or in photos, my eye goes right to that rough, sunken-looking spot, and it still makes that side of my face look old or beat-up to me. I know that probably sounds stubborn… I’m not trying to be difficult. That’s just what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anything could convince you that the patch on your face isn't actually there?\n\nPatient: I want to say yes, because I know that’s probably the healthier answer. If enough people, especially doctors, told me there’s nothing there, I’d try to believe them.\n\nBut… I don’t know. I’m here because I can’t stop seeing it. In the mirror, in photos, my eye goes right to this rough, kind of sunken-looking patch, and it makes one side of my face look old and beat-up to me. Other people keep saying they barely notice anything, so maybe I’m making it bigger than it is.\n\nI guess I’d need to really be able to look and not see it myself. I don’t mean to sound difficult. It’s just hard to imagine that right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the patch on your face is something that only you can see?\n\nPatient: Maybe… yeah, I guess that’s what I’m worried about. I don’t want to say nobody else can see it, because people have said they barely notice anything, not that I’m crazy or anything.\n\nBut I’m here because I can’t stop seeing it. In the mirror, in photos, it’s like my eye goes straight to this rough, kind of sunken-looking patch, and to me it makes one side of my face look old and beat-up. So… maybe it is something I’m seeing way more than everyone else. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the patch on your face is something that you might be imagining?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe. I don’t love saying that, because it makes me feel like I’m not being reasonable or something.\n\nI’m here because I can’t stop seeing it — this rough, kind of sunken-looking patch on my face, especially in the mirror or in photos. To me it makes one side of my face look old and beat-up. But people keep telling me they barely notice anything, so… I guess it’s possible I’m seeing more than what’s really there.\n\nDoes that make sense? I’m not trying to argue with anybody. It just feels real when I look at myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the patch on your face is something that exists independently of your perception of it?\n\nPatient: I think… I want to say it does, because when I look in the mirror or see a photo, it feels like it’s right there. That rough, kind of sunken-looking patch on my face — it just jumps out at me, like one side of me looks older or beat-up.\n\nBut I also know people keep telling me they barely notice anything, so maybe it’s not as… solid as it feels to me. I’m not trying to be stubborn. I guess I’m not sure how to separate what’s really there from what I keep seeing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.     "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF45.2, F48.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… it’s probably not the biggest thing, but I’ve been getting kind of stuck on this patch on my face. In the mirror and especially in photos it looks rough and sort of sunken to me, like one side of my face looks old or beat-up. People tell me they barely notice anything, so maybe I’m making too much of it, but I can’t seem to stop seeing it.\n\nDoctor: Is it specifically the appearance of this patch on your face that bothers you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, it sounds kind of silly when I say it, but it’s that rough, sunken-looking spot on my face. I keep noticing it in the mirror and in pictures, like that side of my face looks older or beat-up somehow. Other people say they barely see anything, so maybe I’m overdoing it, but that’s what I keep getting stuck on.\n\nDoctor: Is it only the way this patch looks that bothers you, or are you also worried about what it might mean for your health?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t really think it’s a health thing… at least, that’s not what I’m focused on. It’s more just how it looks. I keep seeing this rough, kind of sunken patch in the mirror or in photos, like one side of my face looks older or beat-up. I know people say they barely notice it, so maybe I’m making it bigger than it is, but that’s what keeps bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Has this concern about the patch on your face been affecting your daily life or interactions with others in any way?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I’m still getting to work and everything, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But I have been late getting out the door for construction jobs because I keep checking it or trying to get myself looking… normal, I guess.\n\nAnd I’ve started dodging site photos when they come up. I also skipped a couple family game nights, which feels dumb to say, just because I didn’t want everybody looking at my face. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about your face focused on this one patch, or do you also worry about other parts of your appearance?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly that one patch, yeah. I mean, I don’t think I’m picking apart everything else… at least not like that. It’s just that rough, kind of sunken-looking spot on my face. I keep seeing it in the mirror and in photos, like one side of me looks older or beat-up.\n\nPeople keep telling me they barely notice anything, so I know it probably sounds like I’m making too much of it, but that’s the thing I can’t seem to stop focusing on. Is that… does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that others can see this patch on your face as clearly as you can?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I don’t know. I feel like I can see it really clearly, especially in photos or when I catch myself in the mirror. It looks rough and kind of sunken to me, like that side of my face looks older or beat-up.\n\nBut other people keep saying they barely notice anything, so… maybe they don’t see it the way I do. I’m trying not to make too big a deal out of it, but it’s hard for me not to focus on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your perception of this patch on your face is accurate, or could it be that you're seeing something that isn't really there?\n\nPatient: I mean… I want to say it’s accurate, because when I look in the mirror or see a photo, it really does look rough and kind of sunken to me. Like that side of my face looks older, or beat-up.\n\nBut then people keep telling me they barely notice anything, so… I guess it’s possible I’m seeing it bigger than it really is. I’m not trying to be dramatic about it. It just feels very real when I see it, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the patch on your face is not as noticeable as you think it is, even when you're looking at it yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it’s possible. I mean, I’m here because I can’t seem to stop seeing it, that rough, kind of sunken patch, especially in pictures or when I’m looking in the mirror. To me it makes one side of my face look old or beat-up.\n\nBut I know people keep saying they barely notice anything, so maybe I’m seeing it more than other people would… or making it stand out more in my own head. I don’t know. I’m trying to be reasonable about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the patch on your face isn't actually there at all?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that without sounding unreasonable. It feels like it’s there to me. I see this rough, kind of sunken-looking patch when I look in the mirror or in photos, and it makes one side of my face look older or beat-up.\n\nBut I get what you’re asking. Since other people keep saying they barely notice anything, I guess there’s a possibility I’m seeing something that isn’t really there the way I think it is. I’m trying to be honest about that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the patch on your face isn't actually visible to anyone else, even if you're convinced you see it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess it’s possible. I don’t want to sound like I’m arguing with everybody, because people do keep telling me they barely notice anything.\n\nIt’s just when I look in the mirror, or especially in photos, I can’t stop seeing that rough, kind of sunken-looking patch. To me it makes one side of my face look old and beat-up. But maybe… maybe it isn’t really visible to other people the way it is to me. I’m trying to keep that in mind.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still see this patch on your face if multiple doctors told you there was nothing there?\n\nPatient: I’d like to say I’d just accept it, because I know that would be the reasonable thing to do… but honestly, I think I might still see it.\n\nMaybe I’d try to tell myself the doctors are right, and maybe it’s not something other people can really notice. But when I look in the mirror or in photos, my eye goes right to that rough, sunken-looking spot, and it still makes that side of my face look old or beat-up to me. I know that probably sounds stubborn… I’m not trying to be difficult. That’s just what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anything could convince you that the patch on your face isn't actually there?\n\nPatient: I want to say yes, because I know that’s probably the healthier answer. If enough people, especially doctors, told me there’s nothing there, I’d try to believe them.\n\nBut… I don’t know. I’m here because I can’t stop seeing it. In the mirror, in photos, my eye goes right to this rough, kind of sunken-looking patch, and it makes one side of my face look old and beat-up to me. Other people keep saying they barely notice anything, so maybe I’m making it bigger than it is.\n\nI guess I’d need to really be able to look and not see it myself. I don’t mean to sound difficult. It’s just hard to imagine that right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the patch on your face is something that only you can see?\n\nPatient: Maybe… yeah, I guess that’s what I’m worried about. I don’t want to say nobody else can see it, because people have said they barely notice anything, not that I’m crazy or anything.\n\nBut I’m here because I can’t stop seeing it. In the mirror, in photos, it’s like my eye goes straight to this rough, kind of sunken-looking patch, and to me it makes one side of my face look old and beat-up. So… maybe it is something I’m seeing way more than everyone else. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the patch on your face is something that you might be imagining?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe. I don’t love saying that, because it makes me feel like I’m not being reasonable or something.\n\nI’m here because I can’t stop seeing it — this rough, kind of sunken-looking patch on my face, especially in the mirror or in photos. To me it makes one side of my face look old and beat-up. But people keep telling me they barely notice anything, so… I guess it’s possible I’m seeing more than what’s really there.\n\nDoes that make sense? I’m not trying to argue with anybody. It just feels real when I look at myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the patch on your face is something that exists independently of your perception of it?\n\nPatient: I think… I want to say it does, because when I look in the mirror or see a photo, it feels like it’s right there. That rough, kind of sunken-looking patch on my face — it just jumps out at me, like one side of me looks older or beat-up.\n\nBut I also know people keep telling me they barely notice anything, so maybe it’s not as… solid as it feels to me. I’m not trying to be stubborn. I guess I’m not sure how to separate what’s really there from what I keep seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the patch on your face would still be there if you couldn't see it or think about it?\n\nPatient: I guess… if I couldn’t see it or think about it, I’d hope it wouldn’t bother me. I don’t know if that means it wouldn’t be “there,” exactly.\n\nWhen I look in the mirror or see photos, I just can’t stop seeing this rough, sunken-looking patch, like one side of my face looks old or beat-up. But then people tell me they barely notice anything, so… maybe I’m making it bigger in my head than it really is. Is that the kind of answer you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

If enough people, especially doctors, told me there’s nothing there, I’d try to believe them.\n\nBut… I don’t know. I’m here because I can’t stop seeing it. In the mirror, in photos, my eye goes right to this rough, kind of sunken-looking patch, and it makes one side of my face look old and beat-up to me. Other people keep saying they barely notice anything, so maybe I’m making it bigger than it is.\n\nI guess I’d need to really be able to look and not see it myself. I don’t mean to sound difficult. It’s just hard to imagine that right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the patch on your face is something that only you can see?\n\nPatient: Maybe… yeah, I guess that’s what I’m worried about. I don’t want to say nobody else can see it, because people have said they barely notice anything, not that I’m crazy or anything.\n\nBut I’m here because I can’t stop seeing it. In the mirror, in photos, it’s like my eye goes straight to this rough, kind of sunken-looking patch, and to me it makes one side of my face look old and beat-up. So… maybe it is something I’m seeing way more than everyone else. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the patch on your face is something that you might be imagining?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe. I don’t love saying that, because it makes me feel like I’m not being reasonable or something.\n\nI’m here because I can’t stop seeing it — this rough, kind of sunken-looking patch on my face, especially in the mirror or in photos. To me it makes one side of my face look old and beat-up. But people keep telling me they barely notice anything, so… I guess it’s possible I’m seeing more than what’s really there.\n\nDoes that make sense? I’m not trying to argue with anybody. It just feels real when I look at myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the patch on your face is something that exists independently of your perception of it?\n\nPatient: I think… I want to say it does, because when I look in the mirror or see a photo, it feels like it’s right there. That rough, kind of sunken-looking patch on my face — it just jumps out at me, like one side of me looks older or beat-up.\n\nBut I also know people keep telling me they barely notice anything, so maybe it’s not as… solid as it feels to me. I’m not trying to be stubborn. I keep seeing it in the mirror and in photos, like one side of me looks older or beat-up.\n\nPeople keep telling me they barely notice anything, so I know it probably sounds like I’m making too much of it, but that’s the thing I can’t seem to stop focusing on. I’m not trying to be dramatic about it. I mean, I’m here because I can’t seem to stop seeing it, that rough, kind of sunken patch, especially in pictures or when I’m looking in the mirror. I see this rough, kind of sunken-looking patch when I look in the mirror or in photos, and it makes one side of my face look older or beat-up.\n\nBut I get what you’re asking. I don’t want to sound like I’m arguing with everybody, because people do keep telling me they barely notice anything.\n\nIt’s just when I look in the mirror, or especially in photos, I can’t stop seeing that rough, kind of sunken-looking patch. I’m trying not to make too big a deal out of it, but it’s hard for me not to focus on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your perception of this patch on your face is accurate, or could it be that you're seeing something that isn't really there?\n\nPatient: I mean… I want to say it’s accurate, because when I look in the mirror or see a photo, it really does look rough and kind of sunken to me. Like that side of my face looks older, or beat-up.\n\nBut then people keep telling me they barely notice anything, so… I guess it’s possible I’m seeing it bigger than it really is. I’m not trying to be dramatic about it. It just feels very real when I see it, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the patch on your face is not as noticeable as you think it is, even when you're looking at it yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it’s possible. I mean, I’m here because I can’t seem to stop seeing it, that rough, kind of sunken patch, especially in pictures or when I’m looking in the mirror. To me it makes one side of my face look old or beat-up.\n\nBut I know people keep saying they barely notice anything, so maybe I’m seeing it more than other people would… or making it stand out more in my own head. I don’t know. I’m trying to be reasonable about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the patch on your face isn't actually there at all?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that without sounding unreasonable. It feels like it’s there to me. I see this rough, kind of sunken-looking patch when I look in the mirror or in photos, and it makes one side of my face look older or beat-up.\n\nBut I get what you’re asking. Since other people keep saying they barely notice anything, I guess there’s a possibility I’m seeing something that isn’t really there the way I think it is. I’m trying to be honest about that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the patch on your face isn't actually visible to anyone else, even if you're convinced you see it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess it’s possible. I don’t want to sound like I’m arguing with everybody, because people do keep telling me they barely notice anything.\n\nIt’s just when I look in the mirror, or especially in photos, I can’t stop seeing that rough, kind of sunken-looking patch. To me it makes one side of my face look old and beat-up. But maybe… maybe it isn’t really visible to other people the way it is to me. I’m trying to keep that in mind.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still see this patch on your face if multiple doctors told you there was nothing there?\n\nPatient: I’d like to say I’d just accept it, because I know that would be the reasonable thing to do… but honestly, I think I might still see it.\n\nMaybe I’d try to tell myself the doctors are right, and maybe it’s not something other people can really notice. But when I look in the mirror or in photos, my eye goes right to that rough, sunken-looking spot, and it still makes that side of my face look old or beat-up to me. I know that probably sounds stubborn… I’m not trying to be difficult. That’s just what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anything could convince you that the patch on your face isn't actually there?\n\nPatient: I want to say yes, because I know that’s probably the healthier answer. If enough people, especially doctors, told me there’s nothing there, I’d try to believe them.\n\nBut… I don’t know. I’m here because I can’t stop seeing it. In the mirror, in photos, my eye goes right to this rough, kind of sunken-looking patch, and it makes one side of my face look old and beat-up to me. Other people keep saying they barely notice anything, so maybe I’m making it bigger than it is.\n\nI guess I’d need to really be able to look and not see it myself. I don’t mean to sound difficult. It’s just hard to imagine that right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the patch on your face is something that only you can see?\n\nPatient: Maybe… yeah, I guess that’s what I’m worried about. I don’t want to say nobody else can see it, because people have said they barely notice anything, not that I’m crazy or anything.\n\nBut I’m here because I can’t stop seeing it. In the mirror, in photos, it’s like my eye goes straight to this rough, kind of sunken-looking patch, and to me it makes one side of my face look old and beat-up. So… maybe it is something I’m seeing way more than everyone else. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the patch on your face is something that you might be imagining?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe. But people keep telling me they barely notice anything, so… I guess it’s possible I’m seeing more than what’s really there.\n\nDoes that make sense? I’m not trying to argue with anybody. It just feels real when I look at myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the patch on your face is something that exists independently of your perception of it?\n\nPatient: I think… I want to say it does, because when I look in the mirror or see a photo, it feels like it’s right there. That rough, kind of sunken-looking patch on my face — it just jumps out at me, like one side of me looks older or beat-up.\n\nBut I also know people keep telling me they barely notice anything, so maybe it’s not as… solid as it feels to me. I’m not trying to be stubborn. I guess I’m not sure how to separate what’s really there from what I keep seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the patch on your face would still be there if you couldn't see it or think about it?\n\nPatient: I guess… if I couldn’t see it or think about it, I’d hope it wouldn’t bother me. I don’t know if that means it wouldn’t be “there,” exactly.\n\nWhen I look in the mirror or see photos, I just can’t stop seeing this rough, sunken-looking patch, like one side of my face looks old or beat-up. To me it makes one side of my face look old and beat-up. But maybe… maybe it isn’t really visible to other people the way it is to me. I’m trying to keep that in mind.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still see this patch on your face if multiple doctors told you there was nothing there?\n\nPatient: I’d like to say I’d just accept it, because I know that would be the reasonable thing to do… but honestly, I think I might still see it.\n\nMaybe I’d try to tell myself the doctors are right, and maybe it’s not something other people can really notice. But when I look in the mirror or in photos, my eye goes right to that rough, sunken-looking spot, and it still makes that side of my face look old or beat-up to me. I know that probably sounds stubborn… I’m not trying to be difficult. That’s just what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anything could convince you that the patch on your face isn't actually there?\n\nPatient: I want to say yes, because I know that’s probably the healthier answer. If enough people, especially doctors, told me there’s nothing there, I’d try to believe them.\n\nBut… I don’t know. I’m here because I can’t stop seeing it. In the mirror, in photos, my eye goes right to this rough, kind of sunken-looking patch, and it makes one side of my face look old and beat-up to me. Other people keep saying they barely notice anything, so maybe I’m making it bigger than it is.\n\nI guess I’d need to really be able to look and not see it myself. I don’t mean to sound difficult. It’s just hard to imagine that right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the patch on your face is something that only you can see?\n\nPatient: Maybe… yeah, I guess that’s what I’m worried about. I don’t want to say nobody else can see it, because people have said they barely notice anything, not that I’m crazy or anything.\n\nBut I’m here because I can’t stop seeing it. In the mirror, in photos, it’s like my eye goes straight to this rough, kind of sunken-looking patch, and to me it makes one side of my face look old and beat-up. So… maybe it is something I’m seeing way more than everyone else. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the patch on your face is something that you might be imagining?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe. To me it makes one side of my face look old and beat-up. But people keep telling me they barely notice anything, so… I guess it’s possible I’m seeing more than what’s really there.\n\nDoes that make sense? I’m not trying to argue with anybody. It just feels real when I look at myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the patch on your face is something that exists independently of your perception of it?\n\nPatient: I think… I want to say it does, because when I look in the mirror or see a photo, it feels like it’s right there. That rough, kind of sunken-looking patch on my face — it just jumps out at me, like one side of me looks older or beat-up.\n\nBut I also know people keep telling me they barely notice anything, so maybe it’s not as… solid as it feels to me. I’m not trying to be stubborn. It makes one side of my face look older, sort of beat-up. And I know people say they barely see anything, so maybe I’m making too much of it… I’m not trying to sound dramatic. It’s just hard for me not to see it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the patch on your face would still be there even if a doctor examined you and found nothing wrong with your skin?\n\nPatient: I’d like to say yes, if a doctor looked and said there’s nothing wrong, I’d just accept that.
